$

\end{aligned}

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$\boxed{\phantom{23}} \times 0.25 = 1$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 0.25                   &= 1 \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {1} {0.25} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {100} {25} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {25 \times 4} {25} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {25} \times 4} {\cancel {25}} \\\\
a                               &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

The missing number $= 4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

a                               &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The missing number $= 4$
</code></pre>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$64 \div 8 = 32 \div \boxed{\phantom{23}}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
64 \div 8                       &= 32 \div a \\\\
\dfrac {64} {8}                 &= \dfrac {32} {a} \\\\
a                               &= 32 \times \dfrac {8} {64} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {32 \times 8} {64} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {32 \times 2 \times 4} {32 \times 2} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {32} \times \cancel 2 \times 4} {\cancel {32} \times \cancel 2} \\\\
a                               &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

The missing number $= 4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the missing number $= a$

$

\dfrac {64} {8}                 &= \dfrac {32} {a} \\\\
a                               &= 32 \times \dfrac {8} {64} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {32 \times 8} {64} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {32 \times 2 \times 4} {32 \times 2} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {32} \times \cancel 2 \times 4} {\cancel {32} \times \cancel 2} \\\\
a                               &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The missing number $= 4$
</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

<div class='workings'>

\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
    &   2   &   0   &   4   &   6 \\
{}- &       &   3   &   6   &   4 \\

    &   1   &   6   &   8   &   2
\end{array}
$

<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1682$

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A crate holds $8$ cartons of milk. How many crates are needed to hold $349$ cartons?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Crates needed}                          &= \dfrac {\text{Total cartons}} {\text{Cartons per crate}} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {349} {8} \\\\
                                              &= 43 \text{ remainder } 5 \\\\
                                              &\approx 44
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$44 \ \text{crates}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Crates needed}                          &= \dfrac {\text{Total cartons}} {\text{Cartons per crate}} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {349} {8} \\\\
                                              &= 43 \text{ remainder } 5 \\\\
                                              &\approx 44
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$44 \ \text{crates}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Flora is exactly eleven and a half years old. How many months old is she?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Flora's age}                  &= 11 \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text{years} \\\\
                                    &= 11 \dfrac {1} {2} \times 12 \ \text{months} \\\\
                                    &= 11 \dfrac {1} {2} \times 12 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {23 \times 12} {2} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {23 \times 2 \times 6} {2} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {23 \times \cancel{2} \times 6} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
                                    &= 138 \ \text{months}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$138 \ \text{months}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

\begin{aligned}
\text{Flora's age}                  &= 11 \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text{years} \\\\
                                    &= 11 \dfrac {1} {2} \times 12 \ \text{months} \\\\
                                    &= 11 \dfrac {1} {2} \times 12 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {23 \times 12} {2} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {23 \times 2 \times 6} {2} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {23 \times \cancel{2} \times 6} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
                                    &= 138 \ \text{months}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<div class='question question'>

My four pet monkeys collected a pile of $60$ peanuts.

Monkey $A$ woke in the night and ate half of them,

then Monkey $B$ woke and ate one third of what was left, 

then Monkey $C$ woke and ate one quarter of the rest 

and finally Monkey $D$ woke and ate one fifth of what remainder.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working placeholder'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
<div class='working placeholder'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer placeholder'>

</div>
<div class='answer placeholder'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

</code></pre>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many peanuts did Monkey $D$ eat?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey A}          &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 60 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {60} {2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {30 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {30 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                                        &= 30 \\\\
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey B}          &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times (60 - 30) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {30} {3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times 3} {3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                                        &= 10 \\\\
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey C}          &= \dfrac {1} {4} \times (60 - 30 - 10) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 4} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4} \\\\
                                        &= 5 \\\\
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey D}          &= \dfrac {1} {5} \times (60 - 30 - 10 - 5) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {15} {5} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                                        &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Alternate solution.

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey D}          &= \dfrac {1} {5} \times \left(\dfrac {3} {4} \times \left(\dfrac {2} {3} \times \left(\dfrac {1} {2} \times 60 \right) \right) \right) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {1} {5} \times \dfrac {3} {4} \times \dfrac {2} {3} \times \dfrac {1} {2} \times 60  \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {1 \times 3 \times 2 \times 1 \times 60} {5 \times 4 \times 3 \times 2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 2 \times 5 \times 4 \times 3} {5 \times 4 \times 3 \times 2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5 \times \cancel 4 \times 3} {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 4 \times \cancel 3 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
                                        &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey A}          &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 60 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {60} {2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {30 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {30 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                                        &= 30 \\\\
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey B}          &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times (60 - 30) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {30} {3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times 3} {3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                                        &= 10 \\\\
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey C}          &= \dfrac {1} {4} \times (60 - 30 - 10) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 4} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4} \\\\
                                        &= 5 \\\\
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey D}          &= \dfrac {1} {5} \times (60 - 30 - 10 - 5) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {15} {5} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                                        &= 3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Alternate solution.
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey D}          &= \dfrac {1} {5} \times \left(\dfrac {3} {4} \times \left(\dfrac {2} {3} \times \left(\dfrac {1} {2} \times 60 \right) \right) \right) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {1} {5} \times \dfrac {3} {4} \times \dfrac {2} {3} \times \dfrac {1} {2} \times 60  \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {1 \times 3 \times 2 \times 1 \times 60} {5 \times 4 \times 3 \times 2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 2 \times 5 \times 4 \times 3} {5 \times 4 \times 3 \times 2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5 \times \cancel 4 \times 3} {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 4 \times \cancel 3 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
                                        &= 3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</li>

<div class='question subquestion'>

How many peanuts were left in the morning?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of peanuts left in the morning}            &= 60 - 30 - 10 - 5 - 3 \\\\
                                                        &= 60 - 48 \\\\
                                                        &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>36053732-A8EE-4973-8B6C-1D0E4290AC4D</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2:1:8</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g2</p>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
ttime
</li>

<div class='question question'>


Last week George spent $12 \ \text{hours}$ playing on his Xbox. 

He spent the same length of time on it each weekday and twice as long on it each day at the weekend. 

How long (in hours and minutes) did he send on his Xbox on Saturday?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the time spent playing each weekday $= a$

Time spent playing on weekend $= 2a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \times a + 2 \times 2a         &= 12  \\\\
5a + 4a                          &= 12 \\\\
9a                               &= 12 \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac {12} {9} \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac {3 \times 4} {3 \times 3} \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac {\cancel{3} \times 4} {\cancel{3} \times 3} \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac {4} {3} \ \text {hours}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time spent on saturday}    &= 2a  \\\\
                                 &= 2 \times \dfrac {4} {3} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {8} {3} \\\\
                                 &= 2 \dfrac {2} {3} \ \text {hours} \\\\
                                 &= 2 \ \text {hours } 40 \ \text {minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2 \ \text {hours } 40 \ \text {minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the time spent playing each weekday $= a$
<emptyline>
Time spent playing on weekend $= 2a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5 \times a + 2 \times 2a         &= 12  \\\\
5a + 4a                          &= 12 \\\\
9a                               &= 12 \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac {12} {9} \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac {3 \times 4} {3 \times 3} \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac {\cancel{3} \times 4} {\cancel{3} \times 3} \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac {4} {3} \ \text {hours}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time spent on saturday}    &= 2a  \\\\
                                 &= 2 \times \dfrac {4} {3} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {8} {3} \\\\
                                 &= 2 \dfrac {2} {3} \ \text {hours} \\\\
                                 &= 2 \ \text {hours } 40 \ \text {minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$2 \ \text {hours } 40 \ \text {minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write down the decimal number that the arrow is pointing to:

![bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-9-subquestion-1-00.png](/assets/bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-9-subquestion-1-00.png "bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-9-subquestion-1-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$0.7$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write down (in simplest form) the fraction that the arrow is pointing to:

![bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-9-subquestion-2-00.png](/assets/bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-9-subquestion-2-00.png "bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-9-subquestion-2-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \bigg ( \dfrac {3} {10} + \dfrac {1} {2} \bigg ) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \bigg ( \dfrac {3} {10} + \dfrac {5} {2 \times 5} \bigg ) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \bigg ( \dfrac {3} {10} + \dfrac {5} {10} \bigg ) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \bigg ( \dfrac {3 + 5} {10} \bigg ) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \bigg ( \dfrac {8} {10} \bigg ) \\\\
&= \dfrac {8} {2 \times 10} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel{2} \times 4} {\cancel{2} \times 10} \\\\
&= \dfrac {4} {10}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {4} {10}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \bigg ( \dfrac {3} {10} + \dfrac {1} {2} \bigg ) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \bigg ( \dfrac {3} {10} + \dfrac {5} {2 \times 5} \bigg ) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \bigg ( \dfrac {3} {10} + \dfrac {5} {10} \bigg ) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \bigg ( \dfrac {3 + 5} {10} \bigg ) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \bigg ( \dfrac {8} {10} \bigg ) \\\\
&= \dfrac {8} {2 \times 10} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel{2} \times 4} {\cancel{2} \times 10} \\\\
&= \dfrac {4} {10}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {4} {10}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

%empty%

</div>




<ul class='subsubquestion lower-roman question_type_subsubquestion list_type_lower-roman'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

The jug contains water up to the level shown in the diagram. How many millilitres (ml) of water are in the jug?

![bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-9-subquestion-3--subsubquestion-1--00.png](/assets/bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-9-subquestion-3--subsubquestion-1--00.png "bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-9-subquestion-3--subsubquestion-1--00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Water in five divisions}     &= 1 \text {litre} \\\\
\text {Water in one division}       &= \dfrac {1} {5} \\\\
\text {Level of water}              &= \text {Water in 9 divisions} \\\\
                                    &= 9 \times \text {Water in one division} \\\\
                                    &= 9 \times \dfrac {1} {5} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {9} {5} \\\\
                                    &= 1.8 \text {litres} \\\\
                                    &= 1.8 * 100 \text{millilitres} \\\\
                                    &= 1800 \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1800 \text {ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Water in five divisions}     &= 1 \text {litre} \\\\
\text {Water in one division}       &= \dfrac {1} {5} \\\\
\text {Level of water}              &= \text {Water in 9 divisions} \\\\
                                    &= 9 \times \text {Water in one division} \\\\
                                    &= 9 \times \dfrac {1} {5} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {9} {5} \\\\
                                    &= 1.8 \text {litres} \\\\
                                    &= 1.8 * 100 \text{millilitres} \\\\
                                    &= 1800 \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1800 \text {ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

Chan now empties the jug of water by pouring equal amounts into six identical empty beakers. 
One of the beakers is shown in the diagram.

Draw a line on the beaker to indicate the level of water in it.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Water in each beaker}                      &= \dfrac {\text{total water}} {\text{total beakers}} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {1800} {6} \\\\
                                                  &= 300 \text {ml} \\\\
\text {Water in one division of beaker}           &= \dfrac {\text{total capacity}} {\text{number of divisions}} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {400} {8} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {8 \times 50} {8} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel{8} \times 50} {\cancel{8}} \\\\
                                                  &= 50 \\\\
\text{Number of divisions containing 300ml water} &= \dfrac {300} {50} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {50 \times 6} {50} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel{50} \times 6} {50} \\\\
                                                  &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

6th division is to be marked.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Water in each beaker}                      &= \dfrac {\text{total water}} {\text{total beakers}} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {1800} {6} \\\\
                                                  &= 300 \text {ml} \\\\
\text {Water in one division of beaker}           &= \dfrac {\text{total capacity}} {\text{number of divisions}} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {400} {8} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {8 \times 50} {8} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel{8} \times 50} {\cancel{8}} \\\\
                                                  &= 50 \\\\
\text{Number of divisions containing 300ml water} &= \dfrac {300} {50} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {50 \times 6} {50} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel{50} \times 6} {50} \\\\
                                                  &= 6
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
6th division is to be marked.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>
</div>
</li>
</ul>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$20 \%$ of a number is $3.2$. What is the number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $=a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
20 \% \ \text{of} \ a           &= 3.2 \\\\
\dfrac {20} {100} \times a      &= \dfrac {32} {10} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {32} {10} \times \dfrac {100} {20} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {32 \times 100} {10 \times 20} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {16 \times 2 \times 20 \times 5} {2 \times 5 \times 20} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {16 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {20} \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 5 \times \cancel {20}} \\\\
a                               &= 16
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $=a$
<emptyline>

$
\begin{aligned}

a                               &= \dfrac {32 \times 100} {10 \times 20} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {16 \times 2 \times 20 \times 5} {2 \times 5 \times 20} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {16 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {20} \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 5 \times \cancel {20}} \\\\
a                               &= 16
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

Calculate the value of $135 \div (1 + 3 + 5)$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: Using BIDMAS
,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 135 \div (1 + 3 + 5) \\\\
&= \dfrac {135} {1 + 3 + 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {9 \times 15} {9} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel 9 \times 15} {\cancel 9} \\\\
&= 15
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

15

</div>
</div>

\end{aligned}

Hasan correctly worked out that $3 \times 31 \times 73 = 6789$.

What is the value of $6789 \div 31?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Required value}   &= 6789 \div 31 \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {6789} {31} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 31 \times 73} {31} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {31} \times 73} {\cancel {31}} \\\\
                        &= 3 \times 73 \\\\
                        &= 219
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>

<div class='question question'>

$40 \%$ of the children on a school trip are boys and there are $72$ girls. How many children are on the trip?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the total number of children $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(100 - 40) \% \ \text{of} \ a         &= 72 \\\\
\dfrac {60} {100} \times a          &= 72 \\\\
a                                   &= 72 \times \dfrac {100} {60} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {72 \times 100} {60} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {12 \times 6 \times 100} {6 \times 10} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {12 \times \cancel 6 \times 10 \cancel 0} {\cancel 6 \times 1 \cancel 0} \\\\
a                                   &= 12 \times 10 \\\\
a                                   &= 120
\end{aligned}
$

The total number of children $= 120$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$120$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the total number of children $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
(100 - 40) \% \ \text{of} \ a         &= 72 \\\\

a                                   &= 72 \times \dfrac {100} {60} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {72 \times 100} {60} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {12 \times 6 \times 100} {6 \times 10} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {12 \times \cancel 6 \times 10 \cancel 0} {\cancel 6 \times 1 \cancel 0} \\\\
a                                   &= 12 \times 10 \\\\
a                                   &= 120
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The total number of children $= 120$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$120$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

*In this questions, give your answers as fractions **in their simplest form***.

Raj has a bag that contains $8$ blue and $12$ red marbles only.

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What fraction of the marbles in the bag are blue?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction}             &= \dfrac {8} {12 + 8} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {8} {20} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 4} {5 \times 4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel{4}} {5 \times \cancel{4}} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {2} {5}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {2} {5}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction}             &= \dfrac {8} {12 + 8} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {8} {20} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 4} {5 \times 4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel{4}} {5 \times \cancel{4}} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {2} {5}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {2} {5}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Raj takes $2$ blue marbles out of the bag. 

**Now** what fraction of the marbles in the bag are blue?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction}             &= \dfrac {8-2} {20-2} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {6} {18} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {6} {3 \times 6} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel{6}} {3 \times \cancel{6}} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1} {3}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

$\dfrac {1} {3}$

                            &= \dfrac {1} {3}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

If Luke starts with $3.7$, what answer will he get?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 3.7 \times 2 - 5 \\\\
&= 7.4 - 5 \\\\
&= 2.4
\end{aligned}
$

He will get $= 2.4$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2.4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>

\begin{aligned}
&= 3.7 \times 2 - 5 \\\\
&= 7.4 - 5 \\\\
&= 2.4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What number should Luke start with to get an answer of $24?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 - 5          &= 24 \\\\
2a                      &= 24 + 5 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {29} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {29 \times 5} {2 \times 5} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {145} {10} \\\\
a                       &= 14.5
\end{aligned}
$

The number he start with $= 14.5$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$14.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$

a                       &= \dfrac {29} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {29 \times 5} {2 \times 5} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {145} {10} \\\\
a                       &= 14.5
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number he start with $= 14.5$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How long (in hours and minutes) does each person take?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Time} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Speed}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time taken by Rio}   &=  \dfrac{200}{80} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{4 \times 5 \times 10}{4 \times 2 \times 10} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{\cancel{4} \times 5 \times \cancel{10}}{\cancel{4} \times 2 \times \cancel{10}} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{5}{2} \text{ hours} \\\\
                           &=  2\dfrac{1}{2} \text{ hours} \\\\
                           &=  2 \text{ hours } 30 \text{ minutes} \\\\
\text{Time taken by Harry} &=  \dfrac{150 + 150}{100} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{300}{100} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{3 \times 100}{100} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{3 \times \cancel{100}}{\cancel{100}} \\\\
                           &=  3 \text{ hours} \\\\
\text{Time taken by Fahad} &=  \dfrac{200}{60} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{2 \times 2 \times 5 \times 10}{3 \times 2 \times 10} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{2 \times \cancel{2} \times 5 \times \cancel{10}}{3 \times \cancel{2} \times \cancel{10}} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{2 \times 5}{3} \\\\
                           &=  \dfrac{10}{3} \\\\
                           &=  3\dfrac{1}{3} \text{ hours} \\\\
                           &=  3 \text{ hours } 20 \text{ minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Rio takes $2 \text{ hours } 30 \text{ minutes}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Harry takes $3 \text{ hours}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Fahad takes $3 \text{ hours } 20 \text{ minutes}$

</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the area of one face of the cube?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Surface area of cube} = 6 \times \text{edge} \times \text{edge}$


ABBR: $\text{Area of one face of cube} = \text{edge} \times \text{edge}$


Let the edge of the cube $= a$ 

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
6 \times a \times a                       &= 96 \\\\
6 \times a^2                              &= 96 \\\\
a^2                                       &= \dfrac {96} {6} \\\\
a^2                                       &= \dfrac {6 \times 16} {6} \\\\
a^2                                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {6} \times 16} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
a^2                                       &= 16 \\\\
a                                         &= \sqrt {16} \\\\
a                                         &= 4 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Let's find the area of one face of the cube.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of once face}       &= a \times a \\\\
                                &= 4 \times 4 \\\\
                                &= 16 \ \text{cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer placeholder'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>

a^2                                       &= 16 \\\\

a                                         &= 4 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Length of one edge of cube $= 4 \ \text{cm}$

6 \times a^2                              &= 96 \\\\
a^2                                       &= \dfrac {96} {6} \\\\
a^2                                       &= \dfrac {6 \times 16} {6} \\\\
a^2                                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {6} \times 16} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
a^2                                       &= 16 \\\\
a                                         &= \sqrt {16} \\\\
a                                         &= 4 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Length of one edge of cube $= 4 \ \text{cm}$ 
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4 \ \text{cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Yusuf has $6$ equilateral triangles, each with a perimeter of $12 \ \text{cm}$.

He fits them together to make a regular hexagon.

What is the hexagon's perimeter?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of triangle} = 3 \times \text{side}$


ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of polygon} = \text{Sum of all sides}$


*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing starts.*

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


*TODO:: Add two diagram a small equilateral triangle and a hexagon which is made by adding 6 small ones.
Show each side of hexagon is equal to triangle.*

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing ends.*


Let the side of equilateral triangle $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \times a          &= 12 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {12} {3} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 4} {3} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 4} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                   &= 4 \ \text {cm}
\end {aligned}
$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Side of hexagon}                 &= a \\\\
                                        &= 4 \ \text {cm}\\\\
\text {Perimeter of hexagon}            &= 6 \times a \\\\
                                        &= 24 \ \text {cm}                                        
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of triangle} = 3 \times \text{side}$

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \times a          &= 12 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {12} {3} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 4} {3} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 4} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                   &= 4 \ \text {cm}
\end {aligned}
$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Side of larger triangle}         &= 2 \times a \\\\
                                        &= 2 \times 4 \\\\
                                        &= 8 \ \text {cm}\\\\
\text {Perimeter of larger triangle}    &= 3 \times 8 \\\\
                                        &= 24 \ \text {cm}                                        
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>

tratio

</div>
<div class='question question'>

A litre of water wieghs $1 \ \text{kg}$ and a litre of ice weighs $900 \ \text{g}$.

How many more grams will $6 \ \text{litres}$ of water weigh compared to $5 \ \text{litres}$ of ice?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Weight of 6 litres of water}    &= 6 \times 1 \\\\
                                      &= 6 \ \text{kg} \\\\
                                      &= 6 \times 1000 \ \text{g} \\\\
                                      &= 6000 \ \text{g} \\\\
\text{Weight of 5 litres of ice}      &= 5 \times 900 \\\\
                                      &= 4500 \ \text{g} \\\\
\text{Difference}                     &= 6000 - 4500 \\\\
                                      &= 1500 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1500 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Weight of 6 litres of water}    &= 6 \times 1 \\\\
                                      &= 6 \ \text{kg} \\\\
                                      &= 6 \times 1000 \ \text{g} \\\\
                                      &= 6000 \ \text{g} \\\\
\text{Weight of 5 litres of ice}      &= 5 \times 900 \\\\
                                      &= 4500 \ \text{g} \\\\
\text{Difference}                     &= 6000 - 4500 \\\\
                                      &= 1500 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1500 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many people chose water?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of people who choose water}    &= 20 - \dfrac {1} {4} \times 20 - 7 - 30 \% \ \text{of} \ 20 \\\\
                                            &= 20 - \dfrac {20} {4} - 7 - \dfrac {30} {100} \times 20 \\\\
                                            &= 13 - \dfrac {4 \times 5} {4} - \dfrac {30 \times 20} {100} \\\\
                                            &= 13 - \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 5} {\cancel 4} - \dfrac {3 \cancel 0 \times 2 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                            &= 13 - 5 - 3 \times 2 \\\\
                                            &= 8 - 6 \\\\
                                            &= 2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of people who choose water}    &= 20 - \dfrac {1} {4} \times 20 - 7 - 30 \% \ \text{of} \ 20 \\\\
                                            &= 20 - \dfrac {20} {4} - 7 - \dfrac {30} {100} \times 20 \\\\
                                            &= 13 - \dfrac {4 \times 5} {4} - \dfrac {30 \times 20} {100} \\\\
                                            &= 13 - \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 5} {\cancel 4} - \dfrac {3 \cancel 0 \times 2 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                            &= 13 - 5 - 3 \times 2 \\\\
                                            &= 8 - 6 \\\\
                                            &= 2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Maya drew a pie chart to show all their choices. How many degrees should 'Water' have on her pie chart?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Degrees for water on chart}           &= \dfrac {2} {20} \times 360 \ \text{degrees} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 360} {20} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 18 \times 20} {20} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 18 \times \cancel {20}} {\cancel {20}} \\\\
                                            &= 2 \times 18 \\\\
                                            &= 36 \ \text{degrees}
\end{aligned}
$

Water should have $36 \ \text{degrees}$ on the pie chart.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$36 \ \text{degrees}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,

                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 360} {20} \\\\

                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 18 \times \cancel {20}} {\cancel {20}} \\\\
                                            &= 2 \times 18 \\\\
                                            &= 36 \ \text{degrees}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Water should have $36 \ \text{degrees}$ on the pie chart.
</code></pre>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many children did Dr Patel see in July?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Children in July}    &= 240 - 80 \\\\
                            &= 160
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$160$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Children in July}    &= 240 - 80 \\\\
                            &= 160
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$160$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What fraction of the patients seen by Dr Patel in July were children?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Fraction}    &= \dfrac {240 - 80} {240} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {160} {240} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2 \times 80} {3 \times 80} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {80}} {3 \times \cancel {80}} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2} {3}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {2} {3}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Fraction}    &= \dfrac {240 - 80} {240} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {160} {240} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2 \times 80} {3 \times 80} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {80}} {3 \times \cancel {80}} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {2} {3}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {2} {3}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='question question'>

Jada picks one of these cards at random.

$
\large {
\boxed {1} \quad
\boxed {2} \quad
\boxed {3} \quad
\boxed {4} \quad
\boxed {5} \quad
\boxed {6} \quad
\boxed {7} \quad
\boxed {8} \quad
\boxed {9}
}
$

Here are some possible outcomes:

- The number on the card will be a factor of $24$.
- The number on the card will be a multiple of $2$.
- The number on the card will be a multiple of $3$.
- The number on the card will be a multiple of $5$.
- The number on the card will be a square number.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Which of these outcomes $(A, B, C, D, E)$ is most likely to happen?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Probability}  = \dfrac { \text{ Favourable outcomes} } { \text{Total outcomes} }$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{factors of 24}            &= 1, 2, 3,4, 6, 8 \\\\
\text{P(factors of 24)}         &= \dfrac {6} {9} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {3 \times 2} {3 \times 3} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3} \times 3} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {2} {3} \\\\
\text{multiples of 2}           &= 2, 4, 6, 8 \\\\
\text{P(multiples of 2)}        &= \dfrac {4} {9} \\\\
\text{multiples of 3}           &= 3, 6, 9 \\\\
\text{P(multiples of 3)}        &= \dfrac {3} {9} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {3} {3 \times 3} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel{3}} {\cancel{3} \times 3} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {1} {3}  \\\\
\text{multiples of 5}           &= 5 \\\\
\text{P(multiples of 5)}        &= \dfrac {1} {9} \\\\
\text{square numbers}           &= 4, 9  \\\\
\text{P(square numbers)}        &= \dfrac {2} {9} 
\end{aligned}
$

Highest probability $= \dfrac {2} {3}$ for factors of $24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

The number on the card will be a factor of $24$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_probability
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{factors of 24}            &= 1, 2, 3,4, 6, 8 \\\\
\text{P(factors of 24)}         &= \dfrac {6} {9} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {3 \times 2} {3 \times 3} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3} \times 3} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {2} {3} \\\\
\text{multiples of 2}           &= 2, 4, 6, 8 \\\\
\text{P(multiples of 2)}        &= \dfrac {4} {9} \\\\
\text{multiples of 3}           &= 3, 6, 9 \\\\
\text{P(multiples of 3)}        &= \dfrac {3} {9} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {3} {3 \times 3} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel{3}} {\cancel{3} \times 3} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {1} {3}  \\\\
\text{multiples of 5}           &= 5 \\\\
\text{P(multiples of 5)}        &= \dfrac {1} {9} \\\\
\text{square numbers}           &= 4, 9  \\\\
\text{P(square numbers)}        &= \dfrac {2} {9} 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Highest probability $= \dfrac {2} {3}$ for factors of $24$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>The number on the card will be a factor of $24$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<p>2C91B872-1987-4A75-B222-AE5455DF14F1</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2:1:29</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Ayushi Mishra</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_am_pr</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tareaperimeter
</li>
<li>
tsimpleword
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

A rectangle measuring $7 \ \text{cm}$ by $6 \ \text{cm}$ overlaps 
a rectangle measuring $9 \ \text{cm}$ by $8 \ \text{cm}$ as shown in the diagram.

The region shaded grey has an area of $32 \ \text{cm}^2$.

What is the area of the black region?

![bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-29-00.png](/assets/bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-29-00.png "bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-1-question-29-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Overlapped Area}         &= \text {Area of smaller rectangle} - \text {Area of grey region} \\\\
                                &= 7 \times 6 - 32 \\\\
                                &= 42 - 32 \\\\
                                &= 8 \ \text {cm}^2 \\\\
\text {Area of black region}    &= \text {Area of larger rectangle} - \text {Overlapped Area} \\\\
                                &= 9 \times 8 - 8 \\\\
                                &= 72 - 8 \\\\
                                &= 64 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$64 \ \text {cm}^2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_rectangle_expanded
<emptyline>
As per the diagram,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Overlapped Area}         &= \text {Area of smaller rectangle} - \text {Area of grey region} \\\\
                                &= 7 \times 6 - 32 \\\\
                                &= 42 - 32 \\\\
                                &= 8 \ \text {cm}^2 \\\\
\text {Area of black region}    &= \text {Area of larger rectangle} - \text {Overlapped Area} \\\\
                                &= 9 \times 8 - 8 \\\\
                                &= 72 - 8 \\\\
                                &= 64 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$64 \ \text {cm}^2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

# Section D
<ul class='question decimal question_type_question list_type_decimal'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_pr rag_prrl question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>0B0341E6-86C5-4DEF-B25F-46E5C2E9D35D</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2:2:1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>

<div class='question question'>

Here is a sequence of shapes made with grey and white tiles.

![bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-2-question-1-00.png](/assets/bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-2-question-1-00.png "bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-2-question-1-00.png")

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many grey tiles will be there in Shape Number $20?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The number of grey tiles is calculated by adding $1$ to the previous number of grey tiles.

This is an arithmetic sequence too and the difference between the consecutive patterns of the sequence is constant.

The next grey tiles is calculated by adding multiple of $1$ grey tiles to the first pattern and the number of times it is added is based on the position in the sequence.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of grey tiles}   &= 3 + (20 - 1) \times 1 \\\\
                              &= 3 + 19 \times 1 \\\\
                              &= 3 + 19 \\\\
                              &= 22
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$22$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_sequence_find_difference_note
<emptyline>
The number of grey tiles is calculated by adding $1$ to the previous number of grey tiles.
<emptyline>
This is an arithmetic sequence too and the difference between the consecutive patterns of the sequence is constant.
<emptyline>
The next grey tiles is calculated by adding multiple of $1$ grey tiles to the first pattern and the number of times it is added is based on the position in the sequence.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of grey tiles}   &= 3 + (20 - 1) \times 1 \\\\
                              &= 3 + 19 \times 1 \\\\
                              &= 3 + 19 \\\\
                              &= 22
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$22$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many whilte tiles will there be in Shape Number $36?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The number of white tiles is calculated by adding $2$ to the previous number of white tiles.

This is an arithmetic sequence too and the difference between the consecutive patterns of the sequence is constant.

The next white tiles is calculated by adding multiple of $2$ white tiles to the first pattern and the number of times it is added is based on the position in the sequence.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of white tiles}  &= 2 + (36 - 1) \times 2 \\\\
                              &= 2 + 35 \times 2 \\\\
                              &= 2 + 70 \\\\
                              &= 72
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$72$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_sequence_find_difference_note
<emptyline>
The number of white tiles is calculated by adding $2$ to the previous number of white tiles.
<emptyline>
This is an arithmetic sequence too and the difference between the consecutive patterns of the sequence is constant.
<emptyline>
The next white tiles is calculated by adding multiple of $2$ white tiles to the first pattern and the number of times it is added is based on the position in the sequence.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of white tiles}  &= 2 + (36 - 1) \times 2 \\\\
                              &= 2 + 35 \times 2 \\\\
                              &= 2 + 70 \\\\
                              &= 72
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$72$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many tiles will there be in altogether in Shape Number $25?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The number of white tiles is calculated by adding $2$ to the previous number of white tiles.

The number of grey tiles is calculated by adding $1$ to the previous number of grey tiles.

This is an arithmetic sequence too and the difference between the consecutive patterns of the sequence is constant.

The next grey tiles is calculated by adding multiple of $1$ grey tiles to the first pattern and the number of times it is added is based on the position in the sequence.

The next white tiles is calculated by adding multiple of $2$ white tiles to the first pattern and the number of times it is added is based on the position in the sequence.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of white tiles}  &= 2 + (25 - 1) \times 2 \\\\
                              &= 2 + 24 \times 2 \\\\
                              &= 2 + 48 \\\\
                              &= 50
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of grey tiles}   &= 3 + (25 - 1) \times 1 \\\\
                              &= 3 + 24 \times 1 \\\\
                              &= 3 + 24 \\\\
                              &= 27
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total Number of tiles}  &= 50 + 27 \\\\
                              &= 77
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$77$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_sequence_find_difference_note
<emptyline>
The number of white tiles is calculated by adding $2$ to the previous number of white tiles.
<emptyline>
The number of grey tiles is calculated by adding $1$ to the previous number of grey tiles.
<emptyline>
This is an arithmetic sequence too and the difference between the consecutive patterns of the sequence is constant.
<emptyline>
The next grey tiles is calculated by adding multiple of $1$ grey tiles to the first pattern and the number of times it is added is based on the position in the sequence.
<emptyline>
The next white tiles is calculated by adding multiple of $2$ white tiles to the first pattern and the number of times it is added is based on the position in the sequence.
<emptyline>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of white tiles}  &= 2 + (25 - 1) \times 2 \\\\
                              &= 2 + 24 \times 2 \\\\
                              &= 2 + 48 \\\\
                              &= 50
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of grey tiles}   &= 3 + (25 - 1) \times 1 \\\\
                              &= 3 + 24 \times 1 \\\\
                              &= 3 + 24 \\\\
                              &= 27
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total Number of tiles}  &= 50 + 27 \\\\
                              &= 77
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$77$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The difference between $\dfrac{1}{3}$ of a certain number and $\dfrac{1}{4}$ of the same number is $3$. 

What is the number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1} {3} \times a - \dfrac {1} {4} \times a                       &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {3} - \dfrac {a} {4}                                         &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {a \times 4} {3 \times 4} - \dfrac {a \times 3} {4 \times 3}     &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {4a} {12} - \dfrac {3a} {12}                                     &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {4a - 3a} {12}                                                   &= 3 \\\\
4a - 3a                                                                 &= 3 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                                       &= 36
\end{aligned}
$

The number $= 36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1} {3} \times a - \dfrac {1} {4} \times a                       &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {3} - \dfrac {a} {4}                                         &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {a \times 4} {3 \times 4} - \dfrac {a \times 3} {4 \times 3}     &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {4a} {12} - \dfrac {3a} {12}                                     &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {4a - 3a} {12}                                                   &= 3 \\\\
4a - 3a                                                                 &= 3 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                                       &= 36
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number $= 36$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$36$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

$383$ and $6226$ are examples of palindromic numbers as they read the 
same when the order of their digits is reversed. 

Write down the largest five-digit palindromic number that is a multiple of $5$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Unit place digit of multiple of $5$ $= 0, 5$

As per the question,

Number is palindromic so,

Digit at unit place $= $ Digit at $10000$ th place.

The largest five-digit palindromic number that is a multiple of $5 = 59995$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$59995$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The order of the digits is reversed in a certain two-digit whole number. 

This gives a new whole number which is one less than half of the original number. What is the original number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the digit at ones place $= a$

Digit at tens place $= b$

Original number $= 10b + a$

Reversed number $= 10a + b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
10a + b                     &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times (10b + a) - 1 \\\\
10a + b                     &= \dfrac {10b + a} {2} - 1 \\\\
10a + b + 1                 &= \dfrac {10b + a} {2} \\\\
2 \times (10a + b + 1)      &= 10b + a \\\\
20a + 2b + 2                &= 10b + a \\\\
2                           &= 10b - 2b + a - 20a \\\\
2                           &= 8b - 19a \\\\
8b - 19a                    &= 2 \\\\
8b                          &= 19a + 2 \\\\
b                           &= \dfrac {19a + 2} {8}
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out a number that satisfies the above equation.

|  a  |          19a + 2        | Multiple of 8 |
|:---:|:-----------------------:|:-------------:|
| $1$ |  $19 \times 1 + 2 = 21$ |      No       |
| $2$ |  $19 \times 2 + 2 = 40$ |      Yes      |

The digit at ones place $= 2$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The digit at tens place}          &= \dfrac {19 \times 2 + 2} {8} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {40} {8} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 8} {8} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel 8} {\cancel 8} \\\\
                                        &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

The Original number $= 52$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$52$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the digit at ones place $= a$
<emptyline>
Digit at tens place $= b$
<emptyline>
Original number $= 10b + a$
<emptyline>
Reversed number $= 10a + b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
10a + b                     &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times (10b + a) - 1 \\\\
10a + b                     &= \dfrac {10b + a} {2} - 1 \\\\
10a + b + 1                 &= \dfrac {10b + a} {2} \\\\
2 \times (10a + b + 1)      &= 10b + a \\\\
20a + 2b + 2                &= 10b + a \\\\
2                           &= 10b - 2b + a - 20a \\\\
2                           &= 8b - 19a \\\\
8b - 19a                    &= 2 \\\\
8b                          &= 19a + 2 \\\\
b                           &= \dfrac {19a + 2} {8}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out a number that satisfies the above equation.
<emptyline>
|  a  |          19a + 2        | Multiple of 8 |
|:---:|:-----------------------:|:-------------:|
| $1$ |  $19 \times 1 + 2 = 21$ |      No       |
| $2$ |  $19 \times 2 + 2 = 40$ |      Yes      |
<emptyline>
The digit at ones place $= 2$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The digit at tens place}          &= \dfrac {19 \times 2 + 2} {8} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {40} {8} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 8} {8} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel 8} {\cancel 8} \\\\
                                        &= 5
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The Original number $= 52$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$52$
</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2:2:4</p>

<div class='rag'>

talgebra
</li>

</div>
<div class='question question'>

Every digit of a certain positive whole number is either a $3$ or $4$, with each occurring at least one. 

The number divides exactly by both $3$ and $4$. 

What is the smallest number it could be? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using trial and error,

Smallest required number $= 3444$

Let's verify.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {3444} {3 \times 4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 287} {3 \times 4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 4 \times 287} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
&= 287
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3444$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using trial and error,
<emptyline>
Smallest required number $= 3444$
<emptyline>
Let's verify.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {3444} {3 \times 4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 287} {3 \times 4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 4 \times 287} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
&= 287
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3444$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>58BAFA2D-B38E-4A53-8822-FF8913C2BE37</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2:2:5</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Hareni has twice as many sweets as Dora. 
After eating $18$ sweets each, she has five times as many as Dora. How many sweets did Hareni start with?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let at the start number of sweets Dora has $= a$

Number of sweets Hareni has $= 2a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \times (a - 18)       &= 2a - 18 \\\\
5a - 5 \times 18        &= 2a - 18 \\\\
5a - 90                 &= 2a - 18 \\\\
5a - 2a                 &= 90 - 18 \\\\
3a                      &= 72 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {72} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 24} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 24} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                       &= 24
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of sweets Hareni has}       &= 24 \times 2 \\\\
                                         &= 48
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$48$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let at the start number of sweets Dora has $= a$
<emptyline>
Number of sweets Hareni has $= 2a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5 \times (a - 18)       &= 2a - 18 \\\\
5a - 5 \times 18        &= 2a - 18 \\\\
5a - 90                 &= 2a - 18 \\\\
5a - 2a                 &= 90 - 18 \\\\
3a                      &= 72 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {72} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 24} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 24} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                       &= 24
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of sweets Hareni has}       &= 24 \times 2 \\\\
                                         &= 48
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$48$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Three cereal bars, two apples and one tube of mints cost $\pounds 3.14$.

Two cereal bars, three apples and four tube of mints cost $\pounds 4.21$.

What is the total cost of buying one cereal bar, one apple and one tube of mints?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the price of one cereal bar $= c$ 

Price of one apple $= a$

Price of one tube of mint $= m$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
3c + 2a + m             &= 3.14 \\\\
2c + 3a + 4m            &= 4.21
\end{aligned}
$

Let's add both the equations.

$
\begin{aligned}
3c + 2a + m + 2c + 3a + 4m          &= 3.14 + 4.21 \\\\
5c + 5a + 5m                        &= 7.35 \\\\
5 (c + a + m)                       &= \dfrac {735} {100} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \dfrac {5 \times 147} {100 \times 5} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 147} {100 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \dfrac {147} {100} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \pounds 1.47
\end{aligned}
$

Total cost of one cereal bar, one apple and one tube of mint $= \pounds 1.47$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1.47$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the price of one cereal bar $= c$ 
<emptyline>
Price of one apple $= a$
<emptyline>
Price of one tube of mint $= m$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3c + 2a + m             &= 3.14 \\\\
2c + 3a + 4m            &= 4.21
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's add both the equations.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3c + 2a + m + 2c + 3a + 4m          &= 3.14 + 4.21 \\\\
5c + 5a + 5m                        &= 7.35 \\\\
5 (c + a + m)                       &= \dfrac {735} {100} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \dfrac {5 \times 147} {100 \times 5} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 147} {100 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \dfrac {147} {100} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \pounds 1.47
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Total cost of one cereal bar, one apple and one tube of mint $= \pounds 1.47$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 1.47$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Each letter stands for a different number.

The totals for each row and column are shown.

![bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-2-question-7-subquestion-2-00.png](/assets/bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-2-question-7-subquestion-2-00.png "bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-2/section-2-question-7-subquestion-2-00.png")

What is the value of each letter?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the value of A $= a$

Value of B $= b$

Value of C $= c$

Value of D $= d$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
d + d + d + d           &= 26 \\\\
4d                      &= 26 \\\\
d                       &= \dfrac {26} {4} \\\\
d                       &= \dfrac {26 \times 25} {4 \times 25} \\\\
d                       &= \dfrac {650} {100} \\\\
d                       &= 6.5 \\\\
c + c + c + d           &= 17 \\\\
3c + d                  &= 17 \\\\
3c + 6.5                &= 17 \\\\
3c                      &= 17 - 6.5 \\\\
c                       &= \dfrac {10.5} {3} \\\\
c                       &= 3.5 \\\\
b + b + b + c           &= 11 \\\\
3b + c                  &= 11 \\\\
3b                      &= 11 - c \\\\
3b                      &= 11 - 3.5 \\\\
b                       &= \dfrac {7.5} {3} \\\\
b                       &= 2.5 \\\\
a + b + c + d           &= 22 \\\\
a + 2.5 + 3.5 + 6.5     &= 22 \\\\
a + 12.5                &= 22 \\\\
a                       &= 22 - 12.5 \\\\
a                       &= 9.5
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Value of A}           &= 9.5 \\\\
\text{Value of B}           &= 2.5 \\\\
\text{Value of C}           &= 3.5 \\\\
\text{Value of D}           &= 6.5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\text{Value of A} = 9.5$

$\text{Value of B} = 2.5$

$\text{Value of C} = 3.5$

$\text{Value of D} = 6.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the value of A $= a$
<emptyline>
Value of B $= b$
<emptyline>
Value of C $= c$
<emptyline>
Value of D $= d$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>

\begin{aligned}

c                       &= \dfrac {10.5} {3} \\\\
c                       &= 3.5 \\\\
b + b + b + c           &= 11 \\\\
3b + c                  &= 11 \\\\
3b                      &= 11 - c \\\\
3b                      &= 11 - 3.5 \\\\
b                       &= \dfrac {7.5} {3} \\\\
b                       &= 2.5 \\\\
a + b + c + d           &= 22 \\\\
a + 2.5 + 3.5 + 6.5     &= 22 \\\\
a + 12.5                &= 22 \\\\
a                       &= 22 - 12.5 \\\\
a                       &= 9.5
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Value of A}           &= 9.5 \\\\
\text{Value of B}           &= 2.5 \\\\
\text{Value of C}           &= 3.5 \\\\
\text{Value of D}           &= 6.5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\text{Value of A} = 9.5$
